------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between White's testimonies regarding the direction Mia ran. Testimony 7 states Mia ran to the left, while Testimony 8 states she ran to the right. These are directly opposing directions. This creates a spatial contradiction because a person cannot simultaneously run both left and right.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Several spatial propositions are relevant here:

*   **Prop 3:** If Mia ran to the left and Edgeworth gave chase then Edgeworth is far from Mia's initial position.
*   **Prop 6:** If Mia ran to the right then Edgeworth is far from Mia's initial position.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Both Prop 3 and Prop 6 are relevant. White's testimony provides two conflicting accounts of Mia's direction of movement. If Mia ran left (Testimony 7), then Edgeworth would be far from Mia's initial position (Prop 3). If Mia ran right (Testimony 8), then Edgeworth would *also* be far from Mia's initial position (Prop 6). The contradiction isn't about Edgeworth's distance, but about the *direction* of Mia's run, which White cannot consistently describe.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Running inherently implies a direction. A person cannot run in two opposite directions simultaneously. White's conflicting testimonies demonstrate an inconsistency in his observation of this fundamental spatial fact.

The contradiction lies between Testimony 4 ("Her parting gift is this letter, which she tucked safely away in her left breast pocket.") and Evidence 3 (Letter: A letter Kay was carrying. It is addressed to her.). Testimony 4 implies Ms. Crane placed the letter in her pocket, while Evidence 3 states Kay was carrying the letter. This creates a spatial contradiction: if Ms. Crane placed the letter in her pocket, it wasn't being carried by Kay.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 1: The letter from Kay is inside Ms. Crane's left breast pocket and the letter is addressed to Kay then the letter is inside Ms. Crane. This proposition directly addresses the spatial relationship described in the contradiction.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which and explain**

Prop 1 states that *if* the letter is in Ms. Crane's left breast pocket *and* addressed to Kay, *then* the letter is inside Ms. Crane. Testimony 4 asserts the letter was in Ms. Crane's pocket. However, Evidence 3 states Kay was carrying the letter. This contradicts Prop 1 because it implies the letter was *not* inside Ms. Crane, as it was being carried by Kay. The testimony and evidence create an inconsistent spatial scenario.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain**

Not applicable, as Prop 1 directly addresses the spatial contradiction.

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 ("That woman, she came back down on the elevator alone.") and Testimony 2 ("After that, there was no sign of the president getting on the elevator at all!"). These statements directly contradict each other regarding who used the elevator to descend from the roof of the Bigg Building. Testimony 1 states Judge Gavelle used the elevator alone, while Testimony 2 states President Wang never used the elevator.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 2: If President Wang is in front of the elevator then the elevator is behind President Wang. This proposition is relevant. If President Wang *did* use the elevator, he would have been in front of it, and the elevator would have been behind him. However, the testimonies suggest he didn't use the elevator.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 2 applies. The contradiction arises because Testimony 1 implies President Wang was *not* in front of the elevator (since he wasn't using it), and therefore the elevator wasn't behind him. Testimony 2 reinforces this by stating he didn't use the elevator at all. This contradicts the implicit assumption that if someone is descending from the roof, they would use the elevator, placing them in front of it.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

Not applicable, as Prop 2 clearly applies.

**Step 5: Provide your answer in JSON format**
{"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

  "evidence": 2,
  "testimony": 3
}

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states, "But... from under the cherry tree... I feel like... I saw something red." Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet) states that the cherry tree is on the roof of the Bigg Building, specifically on the viewing platform. Testimony 4 states, "Umm... That's right, someone was walking towards me from the other side of that stand." Evidence 7 also states that the candy stand is on the roof, next to the cherry tree. The contradiction arises because Kay saw something red *from under the cherry tree*, but then claims to have seen someone walking from the other side of the candy stand. This implies she was looking *past* the cherry tree, which is inconsistent with being *under* it. If she was under the cherry tree, she would be looking *at* the candy stand, not *past* it.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 2: Cherry tree is inside the viewing platform and the viewing platform is on the roof then Cherry tree is on the roof. This proposition confirms the spatial location of the cherry tree, but doesn't address the contradiction.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 2 confirms the cherry tree's location on the roof. However, the contradiction isn't about the tree's location, but about Kay's perspective *from* under the tree. The testimony implies a specific viewpoint (under the tree) that is incompatible with the subsequent observation (seeing someone past the stand).

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through spatial reasoning. If Kay were truly "under" the cherry tree, her line of sight would be primarily directed towards the candy stand. The testimony describing seeing someone "from the other side of that stand" suggests a different vantage point, one that isn't consistent with being directly beneath the cherry tree. The spatial relationship described in the testimony is incompatible with the implied spatial relationship in the testimony.

Prop 5 states that if the glass of juice is behind the victim, it's behind the chair. However, Testimony 5 implies a premeditated murder, suggesting a more deliberate and planned scene. The seemingly random placement of the juice and bottle, as described in the crime photo, contradicts the idea of a meticulously planned murder. If the scene was carefully staged to look premeditated, the juice and bottle would likely have been positioned differently.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning focuses on the arrangement of objects in the crime scene (as depicted in the photo) and its consistency with the claim of premeditation. A premeditated murder typically involves a deliberate staging of the scene to mislead investigators or to present a specific narrative. The casual placement of the juice and bottle doesn't fit this pattern, suggesting a lack of careful planning.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning is based on the expected consequences of a physical altercation. A struggle would likely result in objects being moved or disturbed. The undisturbed state of the urn contradicts the narrative of a struggle.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Crime Photo 2 (Evidence 3) and Phoenix's Testimony (Testimony 3). Crime Photo 2 states the victim's watch stopped at 3:05. Testimony 3 states that Phoenix heard a loud noise *after* he shoved Doug Swallow and Doug fell on his umbrella. This implies the loud noise and the fall occurred *after* the watch stopped. However, the autopsy report (Evidence 1) states the time of death was 3:00 PM. This creates a spatial and temporal contradiction: the watch stopped at 3:05, but the person was already dead at 3:00 PM. The fall and loud noise, according to Phoenix's testimony, happened *after* death.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 6: If Phoenix Wright shoved Doug Swallow then Doug Swallow moves downward. This proposition aligns with Testimony 3, which states Doug fell after being shoved.
Prop 9: If Doug Swallow falls from above then Doug Swallow moves downward. This proposition also aligns with the general idea of a fall.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 6 is most relevant. Testimony 3 states Doug fell *after* Phoenix shoved him and the watch stopped. However, Evidence 1 (Autopsy Report) states Doug died at 3:00 PM. Prop 6 describes the *result* of a shove (Doug moving downward), but it doesn't account for the temporal discrepancy. The contradiction isn't about the direction of the fall, but *when* the fall occurred relative to the time of death. Phoenix's testimony implies a sequence of events (shove, fall, loud noise) *after* the time of death, which is impossible.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the implied sequence of events in Phoenix's testimony and comparing it to the established time of death from the autopsy report. The contradiction isn't about physical location or movement in a spatial sense, but about the *timing* of events and how they relate to the victim's state (alive vs. dead). The testimony creates a scenario that is spatially and temporally impossible given the evidence.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 2 states that Elise was "with her back to the stone lantern" at the time of the stabbing. Testimony 3 states that Maya stabbed Elise. This creates a spatial contradiction: if Elise's back was to the stone lantern, and Maya stabbed her, then Maya would have had to be positioned in a way that allowed her to stab Elise while Elise's back was turned.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 3: "If Maya hit Elise with the staff then Maya is facing Elise" - This applies because Testimony 0 states Maya hit Elise with the staff.
Prop 6: "If Maya stabbed Elise then Maya is facing Elise" - This applies directly to the stabbing described in Testimony 3.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Both Prop 3 and Prop 6 state that if Maya hit or stabbed Elise, Maya must be facing Elise. Testimony 2 states Elise had her back to the stone lantern during the stabbing. This means Elise was not facing Maya. Therefore, if Prop 6 is true, Maya *must* have been facing Elise. However, if Elise had her back to the stone lantern, Maya could not have been facing her. This contradiction arises from the spatial relationship described in Testimony 2 and the implication of Prop 6.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through spatial reasoning. Testimony 2 establishes a specific spatial relationship: Elise's back to the stone lantern. Testimony 3 describes an action (stabbing) that inherently requires a facing relationship between the attacker and the victim. The two testimonies create an incompatible spatial scenario.

Prop 1 is relevant, but doesn't fully explain the contradiction. While Prop 1 states Godot *could* see Pearl if they were in the same room, it doesn't address the contradiction regarding Godot's arrival and subsequent finding of Pearl. The contradiction isn't about whether they could see each other, but about the implied spatial journey Godot undertook to find Pearl. Testimony 3 suggests a simple arrival, while Testimony 4 implies a search.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction isn't directly about spatial relationships like distance or direction. Instead, it's about the *implication* of spatial relationships. Testimony 3 implies a minimal spatial distance between Godot and Pearl upon Godot's arrival. Testimony 4 implies a greater spatial distance initially, requiring Godot to search for Pearl within the Inner Temple. This difference in implied spatial context creates the contradiction. The contradiction is in the *action* of finding, which implies a search and therefore a non-trivial spatial distance initially.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Evidence 2 (Crime Photo 1) and Testimony 5 (Olga's testimony). Crime Photo 1 describes a dimly lit room with a bottle of grape juice on the table, amidst scattered cards. Testimony 5 states, "He took bottle in his hand... Poor Mr. Smith!" This implies Wright held the bottle *before* the incident. However, Evidence 3 states the bottle bears Wright's *upside-down* prints. If Wright was holding the bottle, the prints would likely be right-side up, not upside down. The spatial arrangement of the scene (bottle on the table) combined with the print orientation (upside down) contradicts Olga's claim that Wright was holding the bottle immediately before the incident. It suggests the bottle was placed on the table *after* Wright's interaction with it, and the prints were left in that position.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 1: If right-hand fingerprints of Wright are on Deadly Bottle then Wright held Deadly Bottle with the right hand. This is relevant, as the prints are described as upside-down, implying a different interaction than simply holding the bottle.

Prop 5: If Deadly Bottle is hit from the front then Deadly Bottle falls backward. This is also relevant, as it suggests the bottle's trajectory and final position.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 1 is key. The evidence states Wright's prints are *upside-down* on the bottle. If Wright had simply held the bottle, the prints would be right-side up. The upside-down prints, combined with the bottle being on the table (as shown in Crime Photo 1), contradict Olga's testimony that Wright was holding the bottle immediately before the incident. Prop 5 is also relevant as it suggests the bottle's position is a result of being hit, which would further contradict Olga's testimony.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves considering the position of the bottle (on the table, as per Crime Photo 1) and the orientation of the fingerprints (upside-down, as per Evidence 3). The natural expectation is that someone holding an object would leave right-side-up fingerprints. The upside-down prints suggest a different scenario – the bottle was placed on the table after Wright interacted with it, and the prints were left in that position. This contradicts Olga's testimony that Wright was holding the bottle.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

L'Belle's testimony (Testimony 3) states he fled in terror as the demon emerged from its prison. Testimony 4 states he fled down the hallway to his right, which has a phone at the end. However, Testimony 0 states L'Belle was hiding in the hallway. This creates a spatial contradiction: if he was hiding in the hallway, how could he simultaneously flee *down* the hallway to his right? The hallway doesn't have two directions - it's a single corridor.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 3: If L'Belle is in the foyer then the phone is far from L'Belle. Testimony 4 states L'Belle fled to the hallway with the phone. This implies he was *not* in the foyer. Testimony 0 states he was hiding in the hallway. This doesn't directly address the contradiction, but it does establish his location.

Prop 7: If L'Belle is in the hallway then the demon is near to L'Belle. Testimony 3 states the demon emerged as L'Belle fled. Testimony 0 states L'Belle was hiding in the hallway. This aligns with Prop 7.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 3 is relevant. Testimony 4 places L'Belle in a hallway with a phone, implying he wasn't in the foyer. Prop 3 states that if he *were* in the foyer, the phone would be far away. The contradiction arises because Testimony 0 places him in the hallway, and Testimony 4 describes him fleeing *from* the hallway, which is inconsistent with the idea of him being in the foyer.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is based on the inherent spatial properties of a hallway. A hallway is a corridor, and corridors generally have a single direction of travel. L'Belle cannot simultaneously be *in* the hallway and fleeing *down* the hallway to his right. The two statements describe conflicting spatial relationships.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 ("Through the window, I did spy, little Ahlbi with my own eye.") and Evidence 5 ("Notice... All of the hallway shutters will remain shut for today's Dance of Devotion."). Andistan'dhin claims to have seen Ahlbi through a window in the hallway, but the notice explicitly states that the hallway shutters were shut, preventing visibility. If the shutters were shut, it would be impossible to see through the windows.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 3: The Treasure Room can be seen from Andistan'dhin's Chamber. This proposition establishes the possibility of seeing the Treasure Room from Andistan'dhin's Chamber.
Prop 6: If Ahlbi passes through the hallway then Ahlbi can potentially see Andistan'dhin's Chamber. This proposition is relevant because it implies a line of sight through the hallway.
Prop 7: If the hallway shutters are shut then Ahlbi cannot pass through the hallway. This is the key proposition. It directly contradicts the possibility of seeing through the windows.

**Step 3: Propositions revealing the contradiction**

Prop 7 directly reveals the contradiction. Andistan'dhin's testimony relies on the ability to see through the hallway windows. However, Evidence 5 states the shutters were shut, which, according to Prop 7, prevents anyone from passing through the hallway and, crucially, seeing through the windows. Therefore, Andistan'dhin's claim of seeing Ahlbi is impossible given the stated condition of the hallway shutters.

**Step 4: Spatial reasoning**

The contradiction is spatial because it concerns the line of sight and the physical barrier (shutters) obstructing that line of sight. The testimony describes a visual observation, while the evidence describes a condition that makes that observation impossible.

  "evidence": 5,

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 ("That's when I saw Juniper dragging a large mat from behind the stage.") and Testimony 3 ("She was heading towards me, so I assume she was on her way to the maintenance area."). This implies Juniper was behind the stage, then moving *towards* O'Conner, who was observing from a vantage point between the stage and the maintenance area. However, Testimony 2 states O'Conner was watching Juniper from this vantage point. If Juniper was dragging a mat *from behind* the stage, and then moving towards O'Conner, it suggests she was initially *behind* the stage, and then moving *towards* the maintenance area, with O'Conner in between. This creates a spatial inconsistency: if O'Conner is between the stage and the maintenance area, and Juniper is moving *towards* the maintenance area, how could O'Conner be watching Juniper *from* that vantage point *while* she's moving away?

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: "If Juniper Woods dragged a large mat from behind the stage then the stage is behind Juniper Woods." This proposition directly relates to the first part of the contradiction.

Prop 5: "If O'Conner is watching Juniper Woods from a vantage point between the stage and the maintenance area then O'Conner can see Juniper Woods." This proposition relates to O'Conner's observation.

Prop 6: "If O'Conner is watching Juniper Woods from a vantage point between the stage and the maintenance area then Juniper Woods is in front of O'Conner." This proposition also relates to O'Conner's observation.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Props 5 and 6, taken together, state that if O'Conner is watching Juniper from a vantage point between the stage and maintenance area, Juniper must be in front of him. However, Testimony 1 and 3 describe Juniper moving *away* from the stage and towards the maintenance area. If she's moving away from the stage, and O'Conner is between the stage and the maintenance area, then Juniper would be moving *away* from O'Conner's line of sight, making it difficult to maintain observation. The testimonies create a spatial scenario that is difficult to reconcile with the vantage point described.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. The testimonies describe a movement pattern that is inconsistent with the described vantage point. If Juniper is moving away from the stage and towards the maintenance area, and O'Conner is positioned between them, it's difficult to maintain a consistent observation.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

However, Cosmos's testimony implies a movement *to* the boarding lounge to observe Terran, which contradicts the autopsy report stating Terran was found *only* in the boarding lounge. The proposition doesn't resolve the contradiction; it highlights the potential for Cosmos to have seen Terran, but doesn't explain why Cosmos would describe a sequence of events implying a later observation.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 ("The bloke wot owned the place was 'oldin' a gun, so 'e should 'ave just fired instead of yellin' at the girl!") and Evidence 9 (Autopsy Report - confirms instant death due to a single bullet wound from the back). Nash's testimony implies Windibank was holding the gun and *yelling* instead of firing. However, the autopsy report states Windibank died from a bullet wound in the back, suggesting he was shot while not actively engaging in a confrontation or holding the gun. This creates a spatial and temporal contradiction: if he was yelling, he wouldn't have been in a position to be shot in the back.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 1: If the bullet is shot from the door direction then the bullet comes from the door direction. This doesn't directly address the contradiction, but it implies a spatial origin of the bullet.
Prop 5: If Nash and Ringo are inside Windibank's and Windibank's is behind Sholmes then Nash and Ringo are behind Sholmes. This is irrelevant to the contradiction.
Prop 9: If Sholmes is hit from behind then Sholmes falls forward. This is irrelevant to the contradiction.

None of the propositions directly address the contradiction between Windibank yelling and being shot in the back.

**Step 3: N/A**

**Step 4: Spatial Reasoning**

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between Windibank's actions (yelling) and the location of the fatal wound (back). Yelling typically implies a forward-facing posture and engagement in verbal communication. Being shot in the back suggests a vulnerable position, unaware of the threat from behind. These two spatial scenarios are incompatible, indicating a discrepancy in the accounts. Nash's testimony paints a picture of Windibank actively confronting someone, while the autopsy report suggests he was caught off guard.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 9, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist**

This contradicts Prop 1. If Prop 1 is true, Shih-na's inability to see Yatagarasu while in the next room is impossible. Therefore, either Yatagarasu wasn't in the next room, or Shih-na wasn't in the next room, or Prop 1 is false. Shih-na's testimony creates a spatial contradiction with Prop 1.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 2 (Furst) and Testimony 3 (Beppo). Furst states he "remember[s] seeing both of the attacker's hands with blood on them." Beppo, however, claims "I, I didn't actually s-see anything myself. No. Not until I heard that scream." This creates a spatial contradiction because if Beppo didn't see anything until *after* the scream, and Furst saw the bloody hands, it implies they had different perspectives and experiences of the event unfolding within the Omnibus. If Beppo was present, he should have seen something, or at least have a recollection of the scene.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 3: If Beppo is inside the Omnibus then the Omnibus is in front of Beppo and the Omnibus is near to Beppo.
Prop 4: If Bruce Fairplay is on the roof deck of the Omnibus then the Omnibus is in front of Bruce Fairplay and the Omnibus is near to Bruce Fairplay.
Prop 5: If Lay D. Furst is on the roof deck of the Omnibus then the Omnibus is in front of Lay D. Furst and the Omnibus is near to Lay D. Furst.

These propositions establish spatial relationships within the Omnibus. Beppo and Furst were both inside the Omnibus, so Prop 3 and Prop 5 apply to them. However, the contradiction isn't about their proximity to the Omnibus, but about their ability to *observe* the event.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

While Props 3, 4, and 5 establish the spatial context of the witnesses within the Omnibus, they don't directly address the contradiction. The contradiction arises from the differing accounts of what they *saw* while being in that spatial location. Beppo's testimony implies a lack of visual observation, while Furst's testimony suggests a clear visual observation of the attacker's hands.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning here is based on the implied perspective of the witnesses. The Omnibus is a confined space. If Beppo was present and attentive, it's reasonable to expect he would have observed at least *something* of the stabbing, even if he didn't see the exact moment. LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 2 states that the defendant (Ellen Wyatt) was "standing in front of the body holding the murder weapon." Testimony 1 states the victim was hit from behind and fell *into* the lantern. These two statements contradict each other spatially. If the victim was hit from behind and fell into the lantern, Ellen could not have been standing in front of the body *while* the victim was being hit. The act of being hit from behind and falling into the lantern would preclude someone from being in front of the body at that moment.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: If Dumas Gloomsbury is hit from the front then Dumas Gloomsbury falls backward.
Prop 5: If Dumas Gloomsbury is hit from behind then Dumas Gloomsbury falls forward.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 5 states that if Dumas Gloomsbury was hit from behind, he would fall forward. Testimony 1 confirms this. Testimony 2 places Ellen in front of the body. This contradicts the spatial consequence of Prop 5. If Gloomsbury fell forward due to being hit from behind, Ellen could not simultaneously be standing in front of him.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is based on the spatial relationship between the action (being hit from behind) and the location of the defendant (standing in front of the body). The act of being hit from behind inherently changes the victim's position, making it impossible for someone to be standing in front of them *during* that action.

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Lance claims he escaped from a room and used an underground passageway to get outside (Testimonies 1, 4, and 5). However, Evidence 12 states that a broken prop sword was used to jam the handle to the hideout's door *from the inside*. This implies someone was *inside* the hideout, actively preventing someone from leaving through the door. If Lance escaped and used the underground passageway, it's contradictory to the evidence suggesting someone was actively blocking the door from the inside.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 2: "If Lance Amano escaped from the room then Lance Amano traveled from the room to the underground passageway." This proposition *supports* Lance's testimony, not contradicts it.
Prop 3: "If the door leading outside was locked then the door cannot be passed through." This proposition is consistent with Lance's testimony about the door being locked.
Prop 4: "If Lance Amano used the underground passageway then Lance Amano traveled from the room to the outside." This proposition also supports Lance's testimony.
Prop 10: "If Lance Amano is inside the room and the room is disconnected from the outside then Lance Amano is disconnected from the outside." This proposition is relevant. If Lance was inside the room and disconnected from the outside, he couldn't have escaped *from* the room. The presence of the broken prop sword suggests someone was actively preventing escape, implying Lance was still inside.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 10 is key here. Lance's testimony describes an escape *from* the room. Evidence 12, however, suggests someone was actively preventing escape *from* the room. This contradicts the idea that Lance was already outside the room when the prop sword was used to block the door. If Lance was still inside, he couldn't have already escaped.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the room, the door, the underground passageway, and Lance's claimed actions. Lance's escape narrative implies he was *outside* the room when he used the passageway. The evidence of the prop sword blocking the door from the *inside* implies he was still *inside* the room at that time. These two spatial positions are mutually exclusive.
